### T000c9ff9cc6f60b766efcd58

Text hash 0ac7475a228b70a0c08914a28fd290a59d3ecf1dd102f7b3bb9ac43cc6362d94; absolute characters 0–373; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10048_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10048_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_6ee4f2ba5b.txt

```text
Wanted: queue:EO:10048:Actual report date of the board created by EO 10048 (Presidential Emergency Board (Railway Labor Act sec. 10): the South ern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines ) and Certain of Its Employees): the board's report to the President and its transmittal letter date; not the order's report deadline, and the 1986 revocation by EO 12553 is only an upper bound.

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10137
Amending E xecutive O rder N o. 100771  
of September 7,1949, Entitled “T rans­
fer of the A dministration  of the 
-  Island of G uam P rom the S ecretary 
of the N avy to the S ecretary of the 
Interior”
By virtue of the authority vested in 
me as President of the United States, 
and finding such action to be in the pub­
lic interest, Executive Order No. 10077 
of September 7, 1949, which transfers 
the adminstration of the Island of Guam 
from the Secretary of the Navy to the 
Secretary of the Interior and revokes 
Executive Order No. 108-A of Decem­
ber 23, 1898, effective as of July 1, 1950, 
is hereby amended by changing the ef­
fective date of such transfer of admin­
istration and such revocation to August 
1, 1950.

June 30, 1950.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite  H ouse,

IF. R. Doc. 50-5847; Filed, June 30, 1950;  
4:25 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10138
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves ­
tigate D ispute  B etween  the T oledo, 
Lorain &  Fairport D ock C ompany  and 
Certain of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Toledo, Lorain &  Fairport Dock Com­
pany, a carrier, and certain of its 
employees represented by the Interna­
tional Longshoremen’ s Association, A. F. 
of L., a labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provi­
sions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive a section of the country of 
essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Toledo, Lorain &  Fairport 
Dock Company or its employees in the 
conditions out of which the said dispute 
arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
July 3, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 50-5884; Filed, July 3, 1950;
12:46 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10139
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves ­
tigate D ispute  B etween  the T oledo 
Lakefront D ock Company and C ertain 
of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Toledo Lakefront Dock Company, a 
carrier, and certain of its employees rep­
resented by the International Longshore­
men’ s Association, Local 158, A. F. of L., 
a labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the Judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive a section of the country of es­
sential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or any 
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Toledo Lakefront Dock 
Company or its employees in the condi­
tions out of which the said dispute arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite  H ouse ,
July 3, 1950.

§  632.21 Disposition of other records  
by the armed forces.  The commanding

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10009
R evoking in  P art E xecutive O rders N o. 
589 of M arch 14,1907, and N o. 1712 of 
F ebruary 24, 1913
WHEREAS Executive Order No. 589 of 
March 14,1907, recited in part that pass­
ports issued by the Government of Ja­
pan to Japanese or Korean citizens who 
were skilled or unskilled laborers to go 
to Mexico, Canada, or Hawaii 'were be­
ing used for the purpose of enabling the 
holders thereof to come to the conti­
nental territory of the United States to 
the detriment of labor conditions there­
in, and ordered that such citizens of Ja­
pan or Korea be refused permission to 
enter the continental territory of the 
United States and 
WHEREAS Executive Order No. 1712 
of February 24, 1913, recited that pass­
ports issued by certain foreign govern­
ments to their citizens or subjects, skilled 
or unskilled laborers, to go to countries 
or places other than the continental ter­
ritory of the United States were being 
used for the purpose of enabling the 
holders thereof to come to the x  conti­
nental territory of the United States to
the detriment of labor conditions there­
in, and ordered that such alien laborers, 
skilled or unskilled, be refused permis­
sion to enter the continental territory 
of the United States; and
WHEREAS the continued restraint 
imposed by the said Executive orders 
upon the freedom of Japanese or Korean 
citizens or subjects who entered the Ter­
ritory of Hawaii under the passports 
referred to above to come to the con­
tinental United States has resulted in 
considerable personal hardship to such 
persons; and
WHEREAS the number of Japanese 
or Korean citizens or subjects thus pro­
hibited from entering the continental 
United States from the Territory of Ha­
waii is so small, and the age of such 
persons is so advanced, that their entry 
into the continental United States would 
no longer be a detriment to labor con­
ditions therein:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 3 of the 
Immigration Act of 1917 (39 Stat. 875; 
8 U. S. C. 136), I hereby revoke the said 
Executive Orders No. 589 of March 14, 
1907, and No. 1712 of February 24, 1913, 
to the extent that they are applicable 
to Japanese or Korean citizens or sub­
jects who entered the Territory of Hawaii 
under the aforesaid passports and who 
are now precluded from legally enterr 
ing or remaining in the continental ter­
ritory of the United States by reason of 
such Executive orders.
The Commissioner of Immigration and 
Naturalization is hereby authorized and 
directed, with the approval of the At­
torney General, to take such action and 
to issue such rules and regulations as 
may be necessary for carrying out the 
provisions of this order.
H arry S. Truman
The W hite H ouse,
October 18, 1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9261, Filed Oct. 18, 1948;
11:50 a. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10010
Creating an E mergency B oard to I nves­
tigate D isputes  B etween  the A kron 
&  Barberton B elt R ailroad C ompany 
and O ther C arriers, and C ertain of 
Their  E mployees
WHEREAS disputes exist between the 
Akron &  Barberton Belt Railroad Com­
pany and certain other carriers desig­
nated in List A attached hereto and made 
a part hereof, and certain of their em­
ployees represented by the sixteen co­
operating railway labor organizations 
designated in List B attached hereto and 
made a part hereof; and 
WHEREAS these disputes have not 
heretofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions jof the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS these disputes, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threaten substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as 
to deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate the said disputes.
No member of the said board shall be 
pecuniarily or otherwise interested in 
any organization of railway employees 
or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
disputes within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by any of the carriers involved or 
its employees in the conditions out of 
which the said disputes arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite H ouse,
October 18,1948.

FEDERAL REGSTER
VOLUME 13 1934* V MitE O  ^ NUMBER 219
Washington, Tuesday, November 9, 1948
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10014  «
Directing Federal Agencies to Cooperate 
W ith S tate and L ocal A uthorities  in  
Preventing P ollution  of Surface and 
Underground W aters
By virtue of the authority vested in me 
as President of the United States, and 
pursuant to the policy expressed, in sec­
tion 1  of the Water Pollution Control 
Act approved June 30, 1948 (Public Law 
845, 80th Congress), of recognizing, pre­
serving, and protecting the primary re­
sponsibilities and rights of the States in 
controlling water pollution, I  hereby di­
rect the heads of the departments, agen­
cies, and independent establishments of 
the executive branch of the Government 
to take such action as may be practicable, 
in cooperation with State and local au­
thorities concerned with control of water 
'pollution, to insure the disposal of sew­
age, garbage, refuse, and other wastes 
accuinulated in the course or as ^ r e ­
sult of Federal activities, and industrial 
or manufactured foodstuffs and 'other 
products destroyed by order or under the 
supervision of Federal regulatory au­
thorities, in such manner as will con­
form with programs formulated under 
State law and applicable to Stat agen­
cies and the public generally for the pres­
ervation and improvement of the qual­
ity of surface and underground waters.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite H ouse,
November 3,1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9872; Filed, Nov. 5, 1948;
2:26 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10015
Amendment of E xecutive O rder N o. 
10010, Creating an E mergency B oard 
To Investigate Disputes  B etween  the 
Akron & B arberton B elt R ailroad 
Company  and Other Carriers, and Cer­
tain of T heir E mployees
By virtue of authority vested in me by 
section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended (45 H  S. C. 160), Executive Or­
der No. 10010 of October 18, 1948, en­
titled “Creating an Emergency Board to
Investigate Disputes between the Akron 
& Barberton Belt Railroad Company and 
Other Carriers, and Certain of Their Em­
ployees,”  is hereby amended as follows:
1 . The following-named carriers are 
hereby added to the list of carriers com­
prising List A attached to and made a 
part of the said Executive order;
The New York & Long Branch Railroad  
Company
Wharton & Northern Railroad
Jersey Central Transportation Company
Grand Central Terminals
State University Railroad Company
Woodstock & Blockton Railway Company
2. The railway labor organizations des­
ignated in List B attached to and made 
a part of the said Executive order as 
“Brotherhood of Railway Carmen of 
America” and “Hotel and Restaurant 
Employes* International Alliance and 
Bartenders’ Int. League of America” are 
hereby designated respectively as “Broth­
erhood Railway Carmen of America” and 
“Hotel & Restaurant Employees and Bar- 
tenders’ International Union.”
This order shall be effective as of Octo­
ber 18, 1948.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite House,
November 5,1948.
[F. R. Doc. 48-9888; Filed, Nov. 8, 1948;
11:17 a. m.j
TITLE 7— AGRICULTURE
Chapter XXI— Organization, Func­
tions, and Procedure
Part 2202—Office of B udget and 
Finance
discontinuance -  of codification
The codification of Part 2202 is hereby 
discontinued. Future amendments to 
description of organization and func­
tions will appear in the Notices section 
of the Federal R egister.
Dated: October 28,1948.
[ seal] R alph S. R oberts,
Acting Director of Finance.
[F. R. Doc. 48-6768; Filed, Nov. 8, 1948;
8:58 a. m.]

«<\O N A Lid/*
VOLUME 15 '9 3 4  <C ^A/JTED ^ NUMBER 152
Washington, Tuesday, August 8, 1950
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10147
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves­
tigate a D ispute ^  Between  the N ew 
York C entral R ailroad C ompany , 
Lines  E ast of B uffalo, and C ertain 
of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the New York Central Railroad Com­
pany, Lines East of Buffalo, a carrier, 
and certain of its employees represented 
by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engi­
neers, Brotherhood of Locomotive Fire­
men and Enginemen, Order of Railway 
Conductors, and Brotherhood of Rail­
road Trainmen, labor organizations; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive a section of the country of essen­
tial transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board 
of three members, to be appointed by 
me, to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pe­
cuniarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of railway employees or any 
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after thff board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the New York Central Railroad 
Company, Lines East of Buffalo, or by 
its employees, in the conditions out of 
which the said dispute arose.
Harry S. T ruman
The W hite H ouse,
August 4, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 50-6959; Piled, Aug. 4, 1950;
4:48 p. m.]
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10148
D esignation  of C ertain O fficers of the
D epartment of C ommerce T o A ct as
S ecretary of C ommerce
By virtue of the authority vested in 
me by section 179 of the Revised Statutes 
of the United States (5 U. S. C. 6), and 
as President of the United States, I 
hereby authorize and direct (1) the Un­
der Secretary of Commerce for Trans­
portation to perform the duties of the 
Secretary of Commerce in case of the ab­
sence, sickness, resignation, or death of 
the Secretary of Commerce and of the 
Under Secretary of Commerce; (2) the 
Assistant Secretaries of Commerce, in 
the order of precedence as determined 
by the dates of their commissions, to 
perform the duties of the Secretary of 
Commerce in case of the absence, sick­
ness, resignation, or death of the Secre­
tary of Commerce, the Under Secretary 
of Commerce, and the Under Secretary 
of Commerce for Transportation; and 
(3) the Solicitor of Commerce to perform 
the duties of the Secretary of Commerce 
in case of fhe absence, sickness, resigna­
tion, or death of the Secretary of Com­
merce, the Under Secretary of Commerce, 
the Under Secretary of Commerce for 
Transportation, and the Assistant Secre­
taries of Commerce.
This order supersedes Executive Or­
der No. 9885 of August 18, 1947, entitled 
“Designation of the Assistant Secretaries 
of Commerce and the Solicitor of Com­
merce to Act as Secretary of Commerce’'.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite H ouse,
August 5, 1950.
[P. R. Doc. 50-6973; Piled, Aug. 7, 1950;
10:56 a. m.]

eral R egister, subparagraph (2) of

»  '  I ITTCO a ’ :
FEDERALREGISTER
VOLUME 15 ' 9 3 4  ^
*  i/A/iTED^ NUMBER 110
Washington, Thursday, June 8,  1950
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10130
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves­
tigate A  Dispute  B etween  the B oston 
&  Albany R ailroad C ompany (N ew 
York C entral R. R. Company , L es­
see) and C ertain of I ts E mployees
WHEREAS a dispute exists between  
the Boston &  Albany Railroad Company  
(New York Central R. R. Company, 
Lessee), a carrier, and certain of its em­
ployees represented by the Brotherhood  
of Railroad Trainmen, a labor organi­
zation; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board,  
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as  
to deprive a section of the country of  
essential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  
authority vested in me by section 10 of  
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board  
of three members, to be appointed by  
me, to investigate the said dispute. No  
member of the said board shall be pecu­
niarily or otherwise interested in any  
organization of railway employees or any  
carrier.
The board shall report its findings to  
the President with respect to the said  
dispute within thirty days from the date  
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this  
date and for thirty days after the board  
has made its report to the President, no  
change, except by agreement, shall be  
made by the Boston &  Albany Railroad  
Company or its employees in the con­
ditions out of which the said dispute  
arose.
Harry S. T ruman
T he W hite H ouse,
June 6, 1950.
[F. R. Doc. 50-4954; Filed, June 7, 1950;
9:25 a. m.]
TITLE 5— ADMINISTRATIVE  
PERSONNEL
Chapter .1 — Civil Service Commission
Part 8— P romotion, D emotion, 
R eassignment  and T ransfer
P art 25—F ederal E mployees ’ P ay 
R egulations
M ISC E L L A N E O U S A M E N D M E N T S
1. Section 8.105 is amended to read as  
follows :
§8.105 Detail — (a) Detail limited to  
6 months.  An employee with a com­
petitive status which has continued for  
at least 6 months may be detailed to a  
position of higher grade or in a different  
line of work for a period not to exceed  
6 months, without prior approval of the  
Commission, provided he meets the ex­
perience and training standards for non­
competitive change to the position.
(b) Detail limited to 3 months.  (1)  
An employee with a competitive status  
may be detailed to a position of higher  
grade or in a different line of work for  
a period not to exceed 3 months without  
prior approval of the Commission and  
without regard to qualifications stand­
ards or to length of the employee’s serv­
ice with competitive status.
(2) Prior approval of the Commission  
must be secured for any renewal or ex­
tension of such a detail beyond 3 months.  
(R. S. 1753, sec. 2, 22 Stat. 403; 5 U. S. C. 631,  
633. E. O. 9830, Feb. 24, 1947, 12 F. R. 1259;  
3 CFR, 1047 Supp.)
2. Effective upon publication in the  
F ederal R egister, subparagraph (1) of 
§  25.11 (e) is amended as set out below.  
As amended, paragraph (e) reads as  
follows :
§  25.11 Definitions. *  *  *
(e) “Waiting period” is the minimum  
time requirement of creditable service  
without an equivalent increase in com­
pensation in order to be eligible for con­
sideration for a periodic step-increase.  
The waiting period is fifty-two calendar  
weeks for grades with step-increases of  
less than $200, and seventy-eight calen­
dar weeks for grades with step-increases  
of $200 or more. Creditable service, in  
the computation of waiting periods, in­
cludes:
(Continued on p. 3541)

- W A U .
DERAL STER
VOLUME 14 ^ U / t u j E O * ^ NUMBER 62
• Washington, Friday, April 1, 1949
TITLE 3— THE PRESIDENT
EXECUTIVE ORDER 10048
Creating an E mergency B oard T o I nves ­
tigate a D ispute  B etween  the S outh ­
ern P acific C ompany (P acific L ines ) 
and Certain of I ts E mployees
Whereas a dispute exists between the 
Southern Pacific C o m p a n y  (Pacific 
Lines), a carrier, and certain of its em­
ployees represented by the Brotherhood 
of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, 
a labor organization; and 
Whereas this dispute has not hereto­
fore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
Whereas this dispute, in the judgment 
of the National Mediation Board, threat­
ens substantially to interrupt, interstate 
commerce to a degree such as to deprive 
a large section of the country of essen­
tial transportation service;
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board 
of three members, to be appointed by 
me, to investigate the said dispute. No 
member of the said board shall be pecu­
niarily or otherwise interested in any 
organization of employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, 
no change, except by agreement, shall 
be made by the Southern Pacific Com­
pany (Pacific Lines) or its employees in 
the conditions out of which the said dis­
pute arose.
H arry S. T ruman 
The W hite H ouse , '
March 30,1949.
[P. R. Doc. 49-2460; Piled, Mar. 30, 1949;
2:25 p. m.]

EXECUTIVE ORDER 10038
C reating  an E m ergency  B oard to  
I nvestigate  D is p u t e B et w e e n th e 
Carriers  R epresented  by  th e E ast­
ern  C arriers ’ C onference  C o m m it t e e, 
W estern  C arriers ’ C onference  C om ­
m itte e and S outheastern  C arriers ’ 
C onference  C o m m ittee, and C ertain  
o f T h e ir E m pl o y ee s
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the carriers represented by the Eastern 
Carriers’  Conference Committee, West­
ern Carriers’  Conference Committee and 
Southeastern Carriers’  Conference Com­
mittee, and certain of their employees 
represented by the Brotherhood of Loco­
motive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor 
organization; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the pro­
visions of the Railway Labor Act, as 
amended; and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate said dispute. No member 
of the said board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organiza­
tion of railway employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the said 
dispute within thirty days from the date 
of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the Board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the carriers represented by the 
Eastern Carriers’  Conference Commit­
tee, Western Carriers’  Committee and 
Southeastern Carriers’  Conference Com­
mittee, or their employees in the con­
ditions out of which the said dispute 
arose.
H arry  S. T ruman
T h e W h it e H ouse ,
February 15, 1949.

11:47 a. in-.]

fer of the A dministration  of the

By virtue of the authority vested in

and finding such action to be in the pub­
lic interest, Executive Order No. 10077 
of September 7, 1949, which transfers 
the adminstration of the Island of Guam

render advisory services for operating

basis of a broad public welfare program 
for financial assistance and case work 
services to the needy aged, needy blind, 
and dependent children. They exercise 
leadership in assisting State welfare de­
partments to use effectively Federal and 
State resources and to develop programs 
not only to meet economic need but also 
to develop and strengthen case work 
services. They advise State welfare de­
partments on legislation, organization,

the order begins on PDF page 6 and its attached lists continue on page 7) is the order itself: it is headed Executive Order 10010, creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act for the Akron & Barberton Belt Railroad Company dispute, and is signed by Harry S. Truman at The White House on October 18, 1948. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF, and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Prior research: a filed claim for this order exists (research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-10-31-apollo-g03.jsonl, line 11) and carries an independent AI whole-case review, but its reasoning described Executive Order 10015 as an amendment without recording it as a later event with a source; this version records it from the Federal Register text of Executive Order 10015 and keeps the end date. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete with no coverage warning and no scope note. The U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order; the Federal Register catalog search found the two instruments recorded here (the amendment of November 5, 1948 and the revocation of February 25, 1986).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by November 17, 1948), and a standstill that runs from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. A held lead copy of the board's own report (a text of PDF pages 1-8 of 70 from the National Mediation Board site, graded held_text_lead, a grade the claim format does not accept as a cited source) carries the date December 17, 1948 on its title page and states that the parties stipulated on the record to extend the time for the board to submit its report to the President until December 17; thirty days after December 17, 1948 is January 16, 1949. These dates are leads and are not recorded as later events, because no claim-grade source and no reviewed factual-event record for the report is held for this order; they are also not claimed as an end, and the clause is described only as functionally spent. The dossier chain, the Federal Register catalog disposition note for this order (amended by Executive Order 10015 of November 5, 1948; revoked by Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986) and my reading of the held texts of Executive Order 10015 and Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25. Executive Order 10015 is an amendment of the attached lists and is recorded as a later event, not as an end.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods it measures here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Savings state none_after_bounded_search; section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the thirty-day report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and the attached List A and List B follow it. The recitals incorporate the lists by reference to identify the carriers and the railway labor organizations, and the lists contain no command; Executive Order 10015 later amended them. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. The ending source cited is the revoking sentence of Executive Order 12553.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"0e7d01f3348a112ba27975fc6869391311216954148ab6abee1d3fe1a41e9ceb","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/c2/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/c2/packet/reviews/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld_after_reveal","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"0e7d01f3348a112ba27975fc6869391311216954148ab6abee1d3fe1a41e9ceb","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/c2/packet/reviews/AFTER_REVEAL.jsonl","pointer":"line:1","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld_after_reveal","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10010#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10010#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"117838559ff84b610ca16bc986f1570765a5f85332a478a45707600352f482f1","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10015.txt","sha256":"117838559ff84b610ca16bc986f1570765a5f85332a478a45707600352f482f1","state":"match"},"sha256":"144846d6cda2b8c9f6722e6a42d7acdde89ef946502246330f45784df894f609","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10015.txt","text_sha256":"117838559ff84b610ca16bc986f1570765a5f85332a478a45707600352f482f1"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"b6574f193c3bd4483f4e6636d324bc44e3dfb7aee6eefe64b6920f5bff030301","path":"jobs/fr-origi Report: NMB report of Emergency Board No. 66, held bytes jobs/ptolemy-custody/output/raw/d36afa6abd56765e684d7bece77ad433cb3d9c39eb03971060b88b14edf669fe.pdf, text jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10010_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10010_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_d3916ac088.txt; the quoted span runs from the cover to the sentence submitting the report, and names the order by order number. Report dated by the cover date (letter date line unreadable or not found; D-20261125-board-cover-date). Clause ends: board 1948-12-17; no-change period 1949-01-16 (inclusive; exclusive 1949-01-15). Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr, later-events:ofr_catalog, all complete with no coverage warning.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order itself contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its report deadline (November 17, 1948, thirty days after signing on October 18, 1948) is a deadline, not an end, and the standstill that runs thirty days after the report is likewise a spent fact under the 2026-10-21 owner ruling; neither is claimed as the end. The actual report date and standstill expiry are not recorded as later events because no reviewed factual-event record for this order's report is in the packet; any report text found in the repository is unreviewed context. The dossier's chain of later events, its three selected machine searches (U.S. Code, eCFR, Federal Register catalog dispositions, each marked complete) and my reading of the held text of Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25, other than the Executive Order 10015 amendment described in the clause reason (an amendment of the attached lists, not an end).\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on the order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board of \nthree members, to be appointed by me, \nto investigate the said dispute. No mem­\nber of the said board shall be pecuniarily \nor otherwise interested in any organiza­\ntion of employees or any carrier.\nThe board shall report its findings to \nthe President with respect to the said \ndispute within thirty days from the date \nof this order.\nAs provided by section 10 of the Rail­\nway Labor Act, as amended, from this \ndate and for thirty days after the board \nhas made its report to the President, no \nchange, except by agreement, shall be \nmade by the Northwest Airlines, Inc., or \nits employees in the conditions out of \nwhich the said dispute arose."},"id":"NA:codex-series-10029-report-record-v1","instrument_id":"EO:10029","legal_effect":"unresolved","producer":"codex-nmb-series-20261025","question_key":"NQ:c3ae6b7a08371900b3a6a90cd5ae6cb0dd5a369e85147c44fffda54802a821d3","schema":"norton-application/v1","scope_id":"EO:10029#whole","state":"ai_reviewed"},"application_review":{"checked_clause_span":{"char_end":2142,"char_start":631,"path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10029.txt"},"checked_evidence_ids":["NE:codex-series-10029-transmittal-v1"],"checks_reviewed":{"applicability":"Exact EO/date/carrier/union and board report/annual case identities must be read together. Truman at The White House on January 19, 1949. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF, and I did not inspect the page images. End: the last-running command of the order is its standstill, which runs from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. A reviewed factual-event record (an independent AI factual review by a reviewer of the same model family as its producer, not a human review) gives 1949-03-10 as the date of the board's signed transmittal letter submitting its report to the President. The letter names Executive Order 10029 of January 19, 1949 and the board's appointment on January 24, 1949, and the board states in it that it submits its report. The held OCR text of the letter garbles the month of its dateline, so the month comes from the reviewer's visual inspection of the page image recorded in the event; it is consistent with the letter's own chronology, because the letter says the board's time was extended thirty days by an order dated February 11, 1949, which moves the report deadline from February 18, 1949 (thirty days after January 19) to March 20, 1949, and March 10 falls inside that period. The extension order itself is not held, and the extension is not claimed as an end. I count the report as made when the board submitted it, on the letter date, so the thirty days after it end on 1949-04-09 (1949 is not a leap year: March 10 plus 30 days is April 9). Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on until-dates the end is recorded inclusive, so the standstill runs through the end of April 9, 1949; the exclusive reading ends it when that day begins. The held sources do not show when the letter reached the White House; if the standstill ran only from receipt, it ended later by the delivery time, and this claim does not establish that interval. Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 revoked the order formally; under the owner ruling of 2026-10-28 that later cleanup revocation is recorded as a later event with its own date and is not the first end. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete with no coverage warning and no scope note; the U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order, and the Federal Register catalog search reports two hits for it while the dossier chain locates one instrument, Executive Order 12553. A search of the held order texts for the number found only the order's own Federal Register text. Prior research: a filed claim by agent claude (research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-10-31-apollo-g03.jsonl) ended the order on 1986-02-25 and a later retag recorded that date only as a not-later-than bound; this claim replaces it with the own-terms end and keeps the revocation as a later event.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are the creation of a board of three members, a bar on members interested in any organization of employees or any carrier, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due February 18, 1949 before the extension the board's letter mentions), and a standstill from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report. The report deadline is a deadline and is not claimed as an end; the end claimed is the expiry of the standstill under the order's own terms, thirty days after the dated submission of the report. The dossier chain, the three machine searches and my reading of the held text of Executive Order 12553 show no revoking, superseding or amending instrument before 1986-02-25. The board's letter says the board was appointed on January 24, 1949 and that its time was extended thirty days by an order dated February 11, 1949; that order is not held and it moves only the report deadline. The retained annual-report text that the factual reviewer mentions reads thirty-six days in one place where the report images read thirty; the review records that the duration is unresolved and accepts no extension duration, and the end claimed here does not depend on it.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the thirty-day period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and ended by its own terms on 1949-04-09.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 was read in full: it consists of an enacting sentence revoking the listed orders and any amendments to them, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of Executive Order 10029 is shown to depend on its continued force after the standstill ended on 1949-04-09; section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently of this order.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence, comprising the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the thirty-day report requirement and the standstill. The signature and date line follow it and contain no command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part. Of the commands, the creation of the board was carried out by the appointment of the board on January 24, 1949 as the board's letter states, and the report requirement was performed by the submission of the report on the letter date. The bar on interested members has no end stated in the order; it applies to the members of this board, and no held source assigns the board any task after its report, so I treat it as spent no later than the end of the standstill, which is the latest dated command. That treatment is an inference, since the date the board was discharged is not shown by any held source.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10029#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10029#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"2e2f9c13ff34e88e3462a43b1ac23a00926af7e8c5e50be5277aade8b0fb785e","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10029.txt","sha256":"2e2f9c13ff34e88e3462a43b1ac23a00926af7e8c5e50be5277aade8b0fb785e","state":"match"},"sha256":"8ac8280e036c696ae42bd90bf95b28e89fedfa0a4de6e589857fca8e9ed0ed0d","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10029.txt","text_sha256":"2e2f9c13ff34e88e3462a43b1ac23a00926af7e8c5e50be5277aade8b0fb785e"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"a71e9407c6ef1b7576b5b229add3f14bba6cd5a9bd6f9a2743fe56a56e6afbd3","path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/afc5c581e901d6a22180aebbd1dcba9cf853d1b4301b339fc05a647b158bbee9/e726ca5f3c289197c5592ae1279cd5530acb72504eb1ee15bc8ab725ac253d5a/selected-pages.txt","sha256":"a71e9407c6ef1b7576b5b229add3f14bba6cd5a9bd6f9a2743fe56a56e6afbd3","state":"match"},"sha256":"afc5c581e901d6a22180aebbd1dcba9cf853d1b4301b339fc05a647b158bbee9","text_path":"jobs/astra-source-retention/output/pages/afc5c581e901d6a22180aebbd1dcba9cf853d1b4301b339fc05a647b158bbee9/e726ca5f3c289197c5592ae1279cd5530acb72504eb1ee15bc8ab725ac253d5a/selected-pages.txt","text_sha256":"a71e9407c6ef1b7576b5b229add3f14bba6cd5a9bd6f9a2743fe56a56e6afbd3"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"}]},{"claim_sha256":"121cba86f5b21b03f0faaa881c3f88 Inventory: one whole-order clause, the dossier span at offsets 4470 to 6429 of the held text, from the order's heading through its last operative sentence (the sentence barring changes in conditions). Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the carriers represented by the Eastern Carriers' Conference Committee, the Western Carriers' Conference Committee and the Southeastern Carriers' Conference Committee, and certain of their employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of railway employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars the carriers represented by those three conference committees or their employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10038 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10038_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10038_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_52bfb598d4.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated September 19, 1949; the board states that the President approved extension requests on February 28, 1949 and on July 27, 1949, the second extending its time to September 19, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking September 19, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on September 19, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to October 19, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10038#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10038#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10038.txt","sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325","state":"match"},"sha256":"957b9dde9cfd9acd1ac9b52b6a3a5d8c898bab6f71c368d4fb03a71bfaf5eabe","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10038.txt","text_sha256":"ee7ae6cba954e57287182eac031dfad2043e31aef3630fe1854e74505b892325"}]}]}

the source URL is the govinfo PDF of the April 1, 1949 issue. Inventory corrected: one clause, offsets 1380 to 2201 of the held text, from the enacting words to the last operative sentence; the dossier's four spans run on past the end of the order into unrelated page text. The order's heading and recitals precede it in the held text and contain no command. Text: The order recites that a dispute exists between the Southern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines), a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Firemen and Enginemen, a labor organization. Acting under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), it creates a board of three members, to be appointed by the President, to investigate the dispute; it bars any board member from being pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of employees or any carrier; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and, as provided by section 10, it bars the Southern Pacific Company (Pacific Lines) or its employees from changing, except by agreement, the conditions out of which the dispute arose, from the date of the order until thirty days after the board has made its report to the President. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held govinfo text layer) has an enacting sentence reading, in part, \"it is hereby ordered that the following Executive Orders, and\" and, on the next line, \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" (the table row for 10048 describes creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording and no exception or saving clause, so the first end is dated on signing, 1986-02-25 (a reading disclosed for the reviewer; the March 3, 1986 printing date is not used). Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10048_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10048_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_6ee4f2ba5b.txt, a held text lead and not an authenticated original) has a cover page dated April 29, 1949 and a letter of transmittal dated San Francisco, Calif., April 29, 1949. The report deadline and the section 10 standstill are spent facts and not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (option C): taking April 29, 1949 as the date the board made its report, the order was functionally spent on April 29, 1949 and the thirty-day period after the report ran to May 29, 1949 (a computed date, thirty days after that cover or transmittal date, not a date shown in a source); the order stayed on the books until the express revocation. The dossier has no citable record covering the report's date line, so the report date is stated here and is not entered as a later event. Prior research: the packet reports no prior claim or review for this order. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the packet with no coverage warning; they are the packet's machine searches.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Earlier-end check: the order's own terms contain a thirty-day report deadline and a standstill running until thirty days after the report, neither of which is an end; no stated fixed end date or termination clause appears in the held text. The packet's bounded number search of 33,118 held texts (clause drafts excluded) found citations only in this order's own printing, the National Mediation Board report copy. The dossier's chain of later events names only Executive Order 12553 as acting on this order, and the three selected machine searches show no revoking, superseding or terminating instrument before 1986-02-25.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the creation of the board, the bar on interested members, the report deadline, the standstill all concern the single dispute the order addressed; none is a separate delegation, standing rule or continuing regulation. The standstill sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, which continues on its own statutory force; the period it measures here was set by this order's creation of the board and nothing held shows it still running.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings: Executive Order 12553 contains no saving clause (the enacting sentence is followed only by the table). The order's one-time commands (creating the board, the report, the status quo period) were exhausted by performance or lapse, section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently and needs no support from this order, and no surviving appointment, transfer or right is shown in any held source. State none_after_bounded_search.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, the table entry matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence carries no exception, so the revocation reaches the whole order and every clause inventoried here.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10048#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10048#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10048.txt","sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5","state":"match"},"sha256":"276e79fa9314ec263ca56e25afb36d55bfac4a7fc2be2183f48751f5c2dedb88","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10048.txt","text_sha256":"532909eb38bcb8ae56235f5af4721bb06a7b8248b30426a7ac5da3bdddd9e1d5"}]}]}

R. Company , L es­\nsee) and C ertain of I ts E mployees\nWHEREAS a dispute exists between  \nthe Boston &  Albany Railroad Company  \n(New York Central R. R. Company, \nLessee), a carrier, and certain of its em­\nployees represented by the Brotherhood  \nof Railroad Trainmen, a labor organi­\nzation; and\nWHEREAS this dispute has not here­\ntofore been adjusted under the provisions \nof the Railway Labor Act, as amended;  \nand\nWHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­\nment of the National Mediation Board,  \nthreatens substantially to interrupt in­\nterstate commerce to a degree such as  \nto deprive a section of the country of  \nessential transportation service:\nNOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the  \nauthority vested in me by section 10 of  \nthe Railway Labor Act, as amended (45  \nU. S. C. 160), I hereby create a board  \nof three members, to be appointed by  \nme, to investigate the said dispute. the registered original is the Federal Register printing of Executive Order 10130 (15 FR 3539; govinfo PDF text layer of the official page, grade original_representation_unverified), headed Executive Order 10130 and signed June 6, 1950. The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the Boston & Albany Railroad Company (New York Central R.R. Company, Lessee) and certain of its employees represented by the Brotherhood of Railroad Trainmen, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10130 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order has no amendment in the held corpus.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:8","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"6b067cb2b9d1ca895a51a7fa83f715f8f67dde108b6d8ee7dfd009452b533611","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g04/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:8","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10130#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10130#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"437a6e7923060568daba379159cfa57836f013309d86f14bbeaeb71e5bc8f5f2","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10130.txt","sha256":"437a6e7923060568daba379159cfa57836f013309d86f14bbeaeb71e5bc8f5f2","state":"match"},"sha256":"bc1167b6679cf0671c124fb62ff31b616814d25eead6a275fb1df02acd5b978d","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10130.txt","text_sha256":"437a6e7923060568daba379159cfa57836f013309d86f14bbeaeb71e5bc8f5f2"}]}]}

The order creates a three-member emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between the Toledo, Lorain & Fairport Dock Company and certain of its employees represented by the International Longshoremen's Association, requires the board to report within thirty days from the date of the order. Inventory: the order is one operative unit; the creation of the board, its thirty-day report deadline and the status-quo sentence restating section 10 are all within the single whole-order clause confirmed here. Ending: Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986 (51 FR 7237, held government printing) revokes the numbered orders in its table, and any amendments thereto; its No. Subject table lists 10138 with a subject line matching this order (creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute). The enacting provision has no stated effective date, so the revocation is read as effective on signing, 1986-02-25; the Federal Register publication date 1986-03-03 and the filing date are not effective dates. The thirty-day or stated report date and the end of any statutory status-quo period are deadlines and spent facts, not ends (owner ruling 2026-10-21 on report deadlines): the order stayed on the books until express revocation. Searches: the OFR executive-order catalog search and the USC and eCFR citation searches (coverage notes read; no coverage warning) show no instrument other than EO 12553 revoking, superseding or amending this order; the held-text citation search found citations of this number only in printings of the order itself, orders printed on the same page, Federal Register index or codification-guide material and (for some) National Mediation Board report front matter.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report deadline is a deadline, not an end (owner ruling 2026-10-21): the order does not end when the board reports, nor when the statutory thirty-day status-quo period after the report runs out; actual report and standstill dates are spent facts that no reviewed event record in this packet supplies, so they are disclosed here rather than recorded as later events. (2) The statutory standstill in section 10 of the Railway Labor Act is the statute's own effect and not a separate command of this order. (3) No later order amends, supersedes or revokes the order before EO 12553 in the held corpus (OFR catalog and number-citation searches). EO 12553 is the first held instrument that revokes it. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Independent commands: the order has no command independent of the board's creation and reporting. The thirty-day report requirement and the status-quo sentence are parts of the same board-creating order and share its end; the status-quo sentence restates section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, whose force comes from that statute.\",\"savings_check\":\"Savings and residual effects: EO 12553's enacting provision contains no savings or exception clause (the ACT record for this pair lists none, and the held text has none). The board's work, report and any agreement reached are completed past events rather than continuing commands of this order; the order created no right, appointment or transfer that survives independently of it, and no held source shows any continuing independent effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Scope: EO 12553 revokes the order by number and the held table row matches its subject; the enacting provision reaches any amendments thereto and has no exception, so it reaches the whole order, not part. The order has no amendment in the held corpus. The dossier lead that each of EO 10138 and EO 10139 is cited by the other arises because both orders are printed on the same Federal Register page (15 FR 4333) and the held text file contains both; neither order's text refers to the other, so no change provision is e1c97e37030ada4468fc353f0992467ccd08da11fe3537a19b716fa3a2b5d5a","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"},{"file_sha256":"ce1c97e37030ada4468fc353f0992467ccd08da11fe3537a19b716fa3a2b5d5a","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/complete-v1/CLAIMS.publishable.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"},{"file_sha256":"ce1c97e37030ada4468fc353f0992467ccd08da11fe3537a19b716fa3a2b5d5a","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/complete-v1/CLAIMS.selected.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"},{"file_sha256":"ce1c97e37030ada4468fc353f0992467ccd08da11fe3537a19b716fa3a2b5d5a","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/applied-v2/CLAIMS.proposed.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"},{"file_sha256":"ce1c97e37030ada4468fc353f0992467ccd08da11fe3537a19b716fa3a2b5d5a","path":"research/readiness/claims/claude-2026-11-02-erasmus-r2.jsonl","pointer":"line:3"}],"reported_material_gaps":[],"research_rationale":"Identity: the registered original is the Federal Register printing of Executive Order 10138 at 15 FR 4241, in the Federal Register of July 4, 1950 (PDF page 3; govinfo text layer, grade original_representation_unverified, recorded as acceptable as original). The page header in the held text reads FEDERAL REGISTER 4241 with the date Tuesday, July 4, 1950; the order is headed Executive Order 10138, is signed July 3, 1950, and its filing line reads Filed, July 3, 1950. The same page also prints Executive Order 10139, which concerns the Toledo Lakefront Dock Company. Text: the order recites a dispute between the Toledo, Lorain & Fairport Dock Company, a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by the International Longshoremen's Association, A. F. of L.; invoking section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, it states \"I hereby create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, to investigate the said dispute.\" It provides that no member of the board may be pecuniarily or otherwise interested in any organization of railway employees or any carrier, directs the board to report its findings to the President \"within thirty days from the date of this order\", and, as provided by section 10 of the Act, states that from the date of the order and for thirty days after the board has reported \"no change, except by agreement, shall be made\" by the company or its employees in the conditions out of which the dispute arose. Inventory: one whole-order clause, from the heading through the last operative sentence, confirmed against the held text. Ending: Executive Order 12553, headed \"Executive Order 12553 of February 25, 1986\" and printed at 51 FR 7237 in the Federal Register of March 3, 1986 (held govinfo text layer), orders that \"the following Executive Orders, and any amendments thereto, are revoked\"; its No. Subject table lists 10138 with the subject \"Creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute\". The enacting sentence has no effective-date wording, so the revocation is dated on signing, 1986-02-25; this is a reading, disclosed for the reviewer, and the publication date of March 3, 1986 is not used. The enacting sentence has no exception or saving clause. Report: the held National Mediation Board copy of the board's report (held text jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_10138_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_10138_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_ba0763e74c.txt, PDF page 1) is titled as the report of the emergency board appointed July 3, 1950 by Executive Orders 10138 and 10139, and its title page is dated at Toledo, Ohio, August 11, 1950. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, the report date and the end of any statutory period are spent facts: the order was functionally spent once the board had reported, but it remained on the books until its express revocation. Searches: I read the coverage notes of the three machine searches before selecting them. The U.S. Code search covers the release point current through 2026-10-09 (laws enacted from then through 2026-10-14 are not reflected) and the eCFR search runs as of 2026-10-14; both are complete with no coverage warning and serve here to show that no current provision gives this order continuing force after its revocation. The OFR disposition-note search records only the Executive Order 12553 revocation for this order. The held-text number search found this number only in Federal Register text files of the same issue (the files for Executive Orders 10136 to 10139 hold the same pages), a Federal Register codification guide for page 4241, and the front matter of the board's report.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Candidate earlier ends examined. (1) The thirty-day report requirement is a deadline, not an end. The board's held report is dated August 11, 1950; that date is disclosed here as the date on which the order became functionally spent, not as its end. (2) The thirty-day period after the report during which no change could be made except by agreement comes from section 10 of the Railway Labor Act; the held sources do not show when the President received the report, so no exact end of that period is asserted, and its running out is a spent fact, not an end. (3) No instrument before Executive Order 12553 amends, supersedes or revokes the order: the OFR disposition notes record only that revocation, and the held-text number search found no other instrument naming the order. No earlier end is shown, so none is claimed.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board it creates. The bar on interested members, the thirty-day report command and the sentence on unchanged conditions all serve the same board and share the order's end. The sentence on unchanged conditions opens by stating that it is made as provided by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, so its force comes from that statute rather than from a separate command of the order.\",\"savings_check\":\"The enacting sentence of Executive Order 12553 contains no saving or exception clause, and the ACT record for this pair lists none. The board's investigation, its report and any settlement that followed are completed past events, not continuing commands of this order; the order created no appointment, transfer or right that survives it, and no held source shows any continuing effect.\",\"scope_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number, its table row matches the order's subject, and the enacting sentence has no exception, so it reaches the whole order. The order has no amendment in the held corpus. The dossier lead that Executive Order 10139 cites this order by number is resolved: the held text file for Executive Order 10139 is the same Federal Register page, which prints both orders one after the other; neither order refers to the other, so no change provision is missing.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"9fea503fcb6a0a237442acdd1a35cec14b467e7bcf22422f2e5da8fa80c3b76f","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:3","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"9fea503fcb6a0a237442acdd1a35cec14b467e7bcf22422f2e5da8fa80c3b76f","path":"research/excavator/claude-erasmus-2026-11-02/r2/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:3","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10138#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10138#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"f64f31b0e582fe272db8ed610f168749f479c4dc274e57cd0cdfa47c40df1d15","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10138.txt","sha256":"f64f31b0e582fe272db8ed610f168749f479c4dc274e57cd0cdfa47c40df1d15","state":"match"},"sha256":"a55ca3dd83cad5db6e88c0fec3b07fc70ab8f8e415f7088acc756540769c37b0","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10138.txt","text_sha256":"f64f31b0e582fe272db8ed610f168749f479c4dc274e57cd0cdfa47c40df1d15"}]}]}
